<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Every nonempty connected semialgebraic bounded open subset of a complex affine variety admitting a properly discontinuous cocompact group of biholomorphisms is smooth and biholomorphic to a bounded symmetric domain. This answers the bounded-domain question of Kollár and Pardon affirmatively.

- **claim-bounded-domain-symmetry** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $U$ be a nonempty connected semialgebraic bounded open subset of a complex affine variety. Suppose that a group $\Gamma$ of biholomorphisms acts properly discontinuously on $U$ and that $U/\Gamma$ is compact. Then $U$ is smooth and is biholomorphic to a bounded symmetric domain.
  - Explanation: Semialgebraicity, boundedness, and a proper cocompact holomorphic action force smoothness and bounded symmetric geometry, even when the ambient variety is initially singular.
- **claim-singular-normal-families** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-singular-montel)): Let $W$ be an open subset of a reduced complex affine algebraic set. The family of holomorphic functions $h$ on $W$ with $\|h\|\leq 1$ is locally equicontinuous. Every sequence in this family has a subsequence converging uniformly on compact subsets to a continuous function $h_\infty$. If $h_\infty(q)=1$ at a point $q\in W$, then $h_\infty=1$ in a neighborhood of $q$. In particular, if $W$ is connected, then $h_\infty\equiv 1$.
  - Explanation: Bounded holomorphic functions remain locally equicontinuous on reduced affine spaces, including nonnormal singularities. Compact limits satisfy a local maximum principle.
- **claim-smooth-single-component** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-smoothness)): If $U$ is not a point, then it is a complex manifold of positive dimension and lies in the smooth locus of a single irreducible component $V$ of the ambient variety.
  - Explanation: Every nonsingleton domain under the standing assumptions lies entirely in the smooth locus of a single ambient irreducible component.
- **claim-proper-intrinsic-distance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-proper-distance)): The function $d_U$ is a finite distance inducing the usual topology of $U$. Every closed $d_U$-ball is compact.
  - Explanation: The intrinsic disc-chain distance is finite, separates points, induces the original topology, and has compact closed balls under the standing assumptions.
- **claim-disc-continuity** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-continuity)): Let $\Omega$ be a smooth ambient coordinate neighborhood, and let $A:[0,1]\times\overline\mathbb D\to\Omega$ be continuous, with each $A_t=A(t,\cdot)$ holomorphic on $\mathbb D$. Suppose $$A_t(\partial\mathbb D)\subset U\quad(0\leq t\leq1), \qquad A_0(\overline\mathbb D)\subset U.$$ Then $A_t(\overline\mathbb D)\subset U$ for every $t$. In particular, this applies to continuous deformations of closed $C^1$ analytic discs; holomorphic extension through the boundary circle is not required.
  - Explanation: A continuous deformation of closed analytic discs stays inside the domain when its boundary remains inside and its initial disc is contained inside.
- **claim-boundary-cell-rank-dichotomy** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-rank-dichotomy)): Let $S$ be a connected real-analytic semialgebraic cell in $V_{\mathrm{reg}}$. Either $$T_pS+iT_pS=T_pV \label{eq:generic-cell}$$ outside an intrinsic null set of $S$, or $S$ is contained in a proper complex algebraic subset of $V$.
  - Explanation: A real-analytic semialgebraic cell either spans the ambient complex tangent space generically or lies in a proper complex algebraic subset.
- **claim-parameter-ramification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-ramified-parameters)): Let $B\subset\mathbb R^d$ be a semialgebraic open set and let $b:B\times(0,t_0)\to\mathbb R$ be a bounded semialgebraic function. There is a null set $E\subset B$ such that, for every $s_*\in B\setminus E$, there are a neighborhood $B_*$ of $s_*$, an integer $l\geq1$, a number $\varepsilon>0$, and a real-analytic function $\widetilde b$ on $B_*\times(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)$ satisfying $$b(s,u^l)=\widetilde b(s,u) \qquad(s\in B_*,\ 0<u<\varepsilon).$$ In particular, $b(s,t)$ has a limit as $(s,t)\to(s_*,0)$ with $t>0$, without any restriction on the relative rates of approach.* *Finitely many such functions can use a common ramification and neighborhood. For countably many functions there is a common full-measure set of good points, with the conclusion applied separately to each function; no common ramification or neighborhood is asserted.
  - Explanation: Away from an exceptional null set, bounded semialgebraic functions admit local analytic extensions after ramifying the scale variable. Finite families share local ramification data; countable families share only a full-measure set.
- **claim-joint-boundary-data** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-boundary-data)): In the coordinates of Equation <a href="#eq:boundary-graph" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:boundary-graph">[eq:boundary-graph]</a>, almost every $s_*\in B$ has the following two properties.* 1. *There are a neighborhood $B_*\subset B$ of $s_*$, a number $\varepsilon>0$, and a real-analytic map $\psi:B_*\times(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)\to\mathbb R^k$ such that $$\psi(s,0)=0,\qquad \psi(s,u)\in A_s\quad(s\in B_*,\ 0<u<\varepsilon).$$* 2. *Set $$E(s,t)=\mathbb R^k\setminus t^{-1}A_s, \qquad d(s,t;v)=\min\{1,\operatorname{dist}(v,E(s,t))\}.$$ There is a $1$-Lipschitz function $d_*:\mathbb R^k\to[0,1]$ such that, for every compact $L\subset\mathbb R^k$, $$\lim_{\substack{s\to s_*\\t\downarrow0}} \sup_{v\in L}\|d(s,t;v)-d_*(v)\|=0. \label{eq:joint-offset-limit}$$ The set $F=\{v:d_*(v)=0\}$ is a closed cone, invariant under every positive real dilation, and $0\in F$.* *In particular, for every sequence $s_j\to s_*$, $t_j\downarrow0$, and $v_j\to v$, one has $d(s_j,t_j;v_j)\to d_*(v)$. If $v\in F$, then $$\operatorname{dist}(v_j,E(s_j,t_j))\longrightarrow0.$$ If $v\notin F$, some fixed ball about $v$ is contained in $t_j^{-1}A_{s_j}$ for all sufficiently large $j$.
  - Explanation: Generic boundary parameters carry analytic interior offsets and locally uniform joint limits of scaled complement distances. Their limiting zero set is a closed dilation-invariant cone.
- **claim-independent-supported-point** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-supported-point)): There are a boundary cell $S$ in the first alternative of Lemma <a href="#lem:rank-dichotomy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:rank-dichotomy">6</a>, a good point $p\in S$, and $k=2n-\dim_{\mathbb R}S$ entire functions $h_1,\ldots,h_k$ on $\mathbb C^N$ such that $$h_j(p)=1,\qquad \|h_j(q)\|\leq\exp(-\|q-p\|^2)\quad(q\in\overline U), \qquad 1\leq j\leq k. \label{eq:peak}$$ The real covectors $$\left.d\log\|h_j\|\right\|_{T_pV}$$ are linearly independent and annihilate $T_pS$. Consequently, in coordinates $(z,w)\in\mathbb C^{n-k}\times\mathbb C^k$ centered at $p$ with $T_pS=\{\operatorname{Im}w=0\}$, their holomorphic logarithmic differentials have the form $$d\log h_j(p)=i\beta_j\,dw,$$ where the $\beta_j$ are linearly independent real rows.
  - Explanation: A measure argument selects a good boundary point with entire peak functions whose real logarithmic differentials span its conormal space.
- **claim-interior-balls-from-graphs** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-disc-family)): Let $\Omega$ be open in a neighborhood of $0\in\mathbb C^k$. Assume that the continuity principle of Lemma <a href="#lem:continuity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:continuity">5</a> holds for continuous deformations of closed analytic discs contained in that neighborhood. Suppose that $f(X,u)$ is an $\mathbb R^k$-valued real-analytic function for real $X$ near $0$ and real $u$ near $0$, that $f(X,0)=0$, and that $$X+i f(X,u)\in\Omega \qquad\text{for all such $X$ and all sufficiently small $u>0$.}$$ Then there are constants $c,C,t_0>0$ and points $a_t\in\mathbb C^k$, for $0<t<t_0$, such that $$\label{eq:disc-balls} \|a_t\|\le Ct, \qquad B(a_t,ct)\subset\Omega.$$ The discs used to obtain these balls stay in an arbitrarily small fixed neighborhood of $0$ after the graph neighborhood is restricted.
  - Explanation: Analytic approaching interior graphs and the continuity principle produce included balls whose radii and center displacements have the same scale.
- **claim-normal-noncollapse** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-noncollapse)): At the point selected in Proposition <a href="#prop:supported-point" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:supported-point">9</a>, the excluded cone in Proposition <a href="#prop:boundary-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:boundary-data">8</a> satisfies $F\ne\mathbb R^k$.
  - Explanation: At the selected supported good point, the excluded cone leaves a nonempty open set of limiting interior directions.
- **claim-two-sided-scaled-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-scaling-limit)): There are an invertible real linear map $L:\mathbb R^k\to\mathbb R^k$ and a vector $Q$ of real quadratic forms on $\mathbb C^m$ such that, with $$R(Z,W)=L(\operatorname{Im}W)-Q(Z),\qquad \mathcal O=\{(Z,W):R(Z,W)\notin F\},$$ the following hold.* 1. *Every compact subset of $\mathcal O$ is contained in $D_t$ for all sufficiently small $t>0$.* 2. *For every $\xi\in\mathbb C^n$ with $R(\xi)\in F$ and every sequence $t_j\downarrow0$, there are $\xi_j\notin D_{t_j}$ such that $\xi_j\to\xi$. These excluded points can be chosen within the scaled coordinate charts.
  - Explanation: Weighted scaling gives both eventual inclusion of compact interior sets and approximation of forbidden points by excluded points. The latter condition is essential for identifying the entire limiting image.
- **claim-global-hermitian-model** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-quadratic-model)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, with $n\ge1$, there are integers $1\le k\le n$, $m=n-k$, a nonempty connected open cone $C\subset\mathbb R^k$, and a vector $H$ of Hermitian forms on $\mathbb C^m$ such that $U$ is biholomorphic to $$\label{eq:model} D=\{(z,w)\in\mathbb C^m\times\mathbb C^k: \operatorname{Im}w-H(z,z)\in C\}.$$ Here $C$ is invariant under every positive dilation, and $H$ is complex linear in its first argument. There are independent real linear functionals $\ell_1,\ldots,\ell_k$ on $\mathbb R^k$ and a constant $c>0$ such that $$\label{eq:strict-supports} \ell_a(y)>0\quad(y\in C),\qquad \ell_a(H(z,z))\ge c\lVert z\rVert^2 \quad(z\in\mathbb C^m),\qquad 1\le a\le k.$$ The original action transfers to a properly discontinuous action on $D$ with compact quotient. No freeness assumption is required.
  - Explanation: Forward and inverse compactness produce a global biholomorphism to a Hermitian quadratic model. Independent positive supports give coercivity and preserve the original compact quotient action.
- **claim-divisible-model-symmetry** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-model-symmetry)): Let $D\subset\mathbb C^m\times\mathbb C^k$ be a connected domain biholomorphic to a bounded domain. Suppose that $D$ contains a point $(0,w)$ and is invariant under $$(z,w)\longmapsto(z,w+a),\qquad (z,w)\longmapsto(e^{i\theta}z,w),\qquad (z,w)\longmapsto(r^{1/2}z,rw)$$ for every $a\in\mathbb R^k$, $\theta\in\mathbb R$, and $r>0$. If $D$ is divisible, it is biholomorphic to a bounded symmetric domain. The case $m=0$ is included.
  - Explanation: Vey's theorem converts divisibility, a bounded realization, and the specified translation, rotation, and dilation invariances into bounded symmetric geometry.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ramified-parameters",
        "exact_quote": "There is a null set $E\\subset B$ such that, for every $s_*\\in B\\setminus E$, there are a neighborhood $B_*$ of $s_*$, an integer $l\\geq1$, a number $\\varepsilon>0$, and a real-analytic function $\\widetilde b$ on $B_*\\times(-\\varepsilon,\\varepsilon)$ satisfying $$b(s,u^l)=\\widetilde b(s,u)\n \\qquad(s\\in B_*,\\ 0<u<\\varepsilon).$$ In particular, $b(s,t)$ has a limit as $(s,t)\\to(s_*,0)$ with $t>0$, without any restriction on the relative rates of approach.*\n\n*Finitely many such functions can use a common ramification and neighborhood.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-boundary-data",
        "exact_quote": "In the coordinates of Equation <a href=\"#eq:boundary-graph\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:boundary-graph\">[eq:boundary-graph]</a>, almost every $s_*\\in B$ has the following two properties.*\n\n1.  *There are a neighborhood $B_*\\subset B$ of $s_*$, a number $\\varepsilon>0$, and a real-analytic map $\\psi:B_*\\times(-\\varepsilon,\\varepsilon)\\to\\mathbb R^k$ such that $$\\psi(s,0)=0,\\qquad\n     \\psi(s,u)\\in A_s\\quad(s\\in B_*,\\ 0<u<\\varepsilon).$$*\n\n\n2.  *Set $$E(s,t)=\\mathbb R^k\\setminus t^{-1}A_s,\n     \\qquad d(s,t;v)=\\min\\{1,\\operatorname{dist}(v,E(s,t))\\}.$$ There is a $1$-Lipschitz function $d_*:\\mathbb R^k\\to[0,1]$ such that, for every compact $L\\subset\\mathbb R^k$, $$\\lim_{\\substack{s\\to s_*\\\\t\\downarrow0}}\n           \\sup_{v\\in L}|d(s,t;v)-d_*(v)|=0.\n     \\label{eq:joint-offset-limit}$$ The set $F=\\{v:d_*(v)=0\\}$ is a closed cone, invariant under every positive real dilation, and $0\\in F$.*\n\n*In particular, for every sequence $s_j\\to s_*$, $t_j\\downarrow0$, and $v_j\\to v$, one has $d(s_j,t_j;v_j)\\to d_*(v)$. If $v\\in F$, then $$\\operatorname{dist}(v_j,E(s_j,t_j))\\longrightarrow0.$$ If $v\\notin F$, some fixed ball about $v$ is contained in $t_j^{-1}A_{s_j}$ for all sufficiently large $j$.",

      },

      "exact_statement": "In the coordinates of Equation <a href=\"#eq:boundary-graph\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:boundary-graph\">[eq:boundary-graph]</a>, almost every $s_*\\in B$ has the following two properties.*\n\n1.  *There are a neighborhood $B_*\\subset B$ of $s_*$, a number $\\varepsilon>0$, and a real-analytic map $\\psi:B_*\\times(-\\varepsilon,\\varepsilon)\\to\\mathbb R^k$ such that $$\\psi(s,0)=0,\\qquad\n     \\psi(s,u)\\in A_s\\quad(s\\in B_*,\\ 0<u<\\varepsilon).$$*\n\n\n2.  *Set $$E(s,t)=\\mathbb R^k\\setminus t^{-1}A_s,\n     \\qquad d(s,t;v)=\\min\\{1,\\operatorname{dist}(v,E(s,t))\\}.$$ There is a $1$-Lipschitz function $d_*:\\mathbb R^k\\to[0,1]$ such that, for every compact $L\\subset\\mathbb R^k$, $$\\lim_{\\substack{s\\to s_*\\\\t\\downarrow0}}\n           \\sup_{v\\in L}|d(s,t;v)-d_*(v)|=0.\n     \\label{eq:joint-offset-limit}$$ The set $F=\\{v:d_*(v)=0\\}$ is a closed cone, invariant under every positive real dilation, and $0\\in F$.*\n\n*In particular, for every sequence $s_j\\to s_*$, $t_j\\downarrow0$, and $v_j\\to v$, one has $d(s_j,t_j;v_j)\\to d_*(v)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-supported-point",
        "exact_quote": "There are a boundary cell $S$ in the first alternative of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:rank-dichotomy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:rank-dichotomy\">6</a>, a good point $p\\in S$, and $k=2n-\\dim_{\\mathbb R}S$ entire functions $h_1,\\ldots,h_k$ on $\\mathbb C^N$ such that $$h_j(p)=1,\\qquad\n |h_j(q)|\\leq\\exp(-|q-p|^2)\\quad(q\\in\\overline U),\n \\qquad 1\\leq j\\leq k.\n \\label{eq:peak}$$ The real covectors $$\\left.d\\log|h_j|\\right|_{T_pV}$$ are linearly independent and annihilate $T_pS$.         "source_id": "PM-058.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-disc-family",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Omega$ be open in a neighborhood of $0\\in\\mathbb C^k$. Assume that the continuity principle of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:continuity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:continuity\">5</a> holds for continuous deformations of closed analytic discs contained in that neighborhood.       },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Bounded symmetric domain** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A bounded symmetric domain is a bounded domain in a complex vector space with a holomorphic involution at every point for which that point is an isolated fixed point.
- **Affine variety convention** ([source](#sec:introduction)): We regard an affine variety as a reduced complex algebraic set, and fix a closed algebraic embedding into some $\mathbb C^N$.
- **Semialgebraic subset** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A subset is semialgebraic if it is given by a finite Boolean combination of polynomial equalities and inequalities in the real and imaginary coordinates.
- **Normal-offset fibers** ([source](#sec:preparation)): Choose $\rho>0$ so that the points with $s\in B$ and $\|v\|<\rho$ below stay in the coordinate patch, and put $$\begin{split} v&=\operatorname{Im}w_0-\phi(s),\qquad s=(z_0,x_0),\quad x_0=\operatorname{Re}w_0,\\ A_s&=\{v\in\mathbb R^k:\|v\|<\rho,\ (z_0,x_0+i(\phi(s)+v))\in U\}. \end{split} \label{eq:offset-fibers}$$
- **Excluded cone** ([source](#stmt-prop-boundary-data)): The set $F=\{v:d_*(v)=0\}$ is a closed cone, invariant under every positive real dilation, and $0\in F$.
- **Normalized harmonic conjugation operator** ([source](#eq:disc-contraction)): Let $T$ be the bounded conjugation operator which recovers the real part of a holomorphic boundary value from its imaginary part, with real part of mean zero, and put $$\mathcal T Y=TY-(TY)(0).$$
- **Preliminary quadratic model** ([source](#eq:preliminary-model)): $$\label{eq:preliminary-model} P_2=L^{-1}Q,\qquad D_0=\{(Z,W):\operatorname{Im}W-P_2(Z)\in C\}.$$
- **Divisible domain** ([source](#sec:symmetry)): A domain is *divisible* if a group of holomorphic automorphisms acts properly when given the discrete topology and has a compact covering set.

| q-ambient-distance-lower-bound | $d_U(x,y)$ | ≥ | $c\\|x-y\\|$ | The constant depends on bounds for the ambient coordinate functions. |
| q-limiting-distance-lipschitz | Lipschitz constant of $d_*$ | ≤ | $1$ | At almost every boundary parameter in the chosen graph coordinates. |
| q-global-peak-decay | magnitude of the evaluated coordinate function | ≤ | $\exp(-\|q-p\|^2)$ | The selected good boundary point and its independent supports. |
| q-interior-ball-center | magnitude of the indexed sequence term | ≤ | $Ct$ | Sufficiently small positive scale, after fixing the graph neighborhood and disc parameters. |
| q-hermitian-support-coercivity | value of the linear functional evaluated on the diagonal form | ≥ | $c\lVert z\rVert^2$ | A common positive constant for all tangential vectors and selected supports. |
| q-bounded-realization-tangential-radius | norm of the scaled complex variable | ≤ | $\frac{1}{2\sqrt c}$ | All model points; the normal coordinate is obtained from the first support functional. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-singular-compactness** [claim](#stmt-lem-singular-montel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-singular-montel): Finite projections, symmetric polynomials of sheet values, Cauchy estimates, and the maximum principle. -> Local equicontinuity, compact subsequences, and a maximum principle for limits.
- **step-remove-singularities** [claim](#stmt-prop-smoothness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-smoothness): A polynomial-weighted peak localizes cocompact automorphism sequences at a smooth ambient point. -> Smoothness in a single irreducible component.
- **step-proper-distance** [claim](#stmt-prop-proper-distance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-proper-distance): Coordinate Schwarz bounds and cocompact translation give uniform compact local balls; disc chains extend compactness to every radius. -> A finite proper intrinsic distance with the original topology.
- **step-disc-continuity** [claim](#stmt-lem-continuity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-continuity): Schwarz contraction and proper intrinsic balls keep interior disc values in compact subsets during limiting deformations. -> The entire deformation remains inside the domain.
- **step-cell-rank** [claim](#stmt-lem-rank-dichotomy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rank-dichotomy): Complexification of algebraic analytic parametrizations identifies generic differential rank with the dimension of the complex Zariski closure. -> Generic normal graph cells or proper algebraic exceptional subsets.
- **step-ramify-parameters** [claim](#stmt-lem-ramified-parameters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ramified-parameters): Cylindrical decomposition, finite monodromy, ramification, and boundedness remove the scale singularity generically. -> Local analytic ramification and unrestricted joint limits outside a null set.
- **step-joint-boundary-limits** [claim](#stmt-prop-boundary-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-boundary-data): Semialgebraic selection supplies offsets; rational distance tests and Lipschitz extension give compact-uniform joint limits. -> Analytic interior graphs and an excluded cone at generic boundary points.
- **step-independent-supports** [claim](#stmt-prop-supported-point) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-supported-point): Vary weighted maxima over ambient linear terms and use Fubini on critical-pair bundles to select independent conormals at a good point. -> A good point carrying independent entire peak functions.
- **step-disc-interior-balls** [claim](#stmt-lem-disc-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-disc-family): Solve the harmonic-conjugation boundary equation by contraction, regularize discs positively, and use an invertible limiting evaluation differential. -> Included balls with radius comparable to center displacement.
- **step-normal-noncollapse** [claim](#stmt-prop-noncollapse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-noncollapse): Straighten a totally real slice, construct interior balls, and contradict vanishing limiting complement distance. -> A nonempty open set of limiting normal interior directions.
- **step-two-sided-scaling** [claim](#stmt-prop-scaling-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-scaling-limit): Taylor-expand the old offset under weighted dilation and apply joint distance limits to both interior and excluded moving points. -> Compact interior inclusion and excluded-point approximation for a quadratic residual.
- **step-forward-normalization** [claim](#stmt-thm-quadratic-model) / [proof](#pm-h-14): Cocompact normalization and independent peak estimates bound every scaled normal coordinate and produce a forward holomorphic limit. -> A locally uniform limit of normalized forward maps.
- **step-inverse-compactness** [claim](#stmt-thm-quadratic-model) / [proof](#pm-h-15): Fixed disc chains in the model and proper intrinsic balls give compact containment for inverse maps. -> An inverse limit mapping into the original domain and a composition identity.
- **step-global-image-identification** [claim](#stmt-thm-quadratic-model) / [proof](#pm-h-16): The zero-free Jacobian limit principle and stable local image balls exclude forbidden residuals; connectedness and inverse composition identify the global image. -> A global biholomorphism onto the chosen model component.
- **step-hermitian-model-supports** [claim](#stmt-thm-quadratic-model) / [proof](#pm-h-17): Remove holomorphic quadratic terms by a triangular change, average peak inequalities over scalar rotations, and transfer the original action by conjugacy. -> The Hermitian model with strict positive supports and a proper cocompact action.
- **step-vey-symmetry** [claim](#stmt-thm-model-symmetry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-model-symmetry): Construct a bounded Cayley realization and verify translation, rotation, dilation, and divisibility hypotheses before invoking Vey's theorem. -> Biholomorphic equivalence to a bounded symmetric domain.
- **step-main-classification** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-model-symmetry): Combine smoothness and positive-dimensional model symmetry with the singleton case. -> The stated classification of the original domain.

          "exact_quote": "Let $B\\subset\\mathbb R^d$ be a semialgebraic open set and let $b:B\\times(0,t_0)\\to\\mathbb R$ be a bounded semialgebraic function. There is a null set $E\\subset B$ such that, for every $s_*\\in B\\setminus E$, there are a neighborhood $B_*$ of $s_*$, an integer $l\\geq1$, a number $\\varepsilon>0$, and a real-analytic function $\\widetilde b$ on $B_*\\times(-\\varepsilon,\\varepsilon)$ satisfying $$b(s,u^l)=\\widetilde b(s,u)\n \\qquad(s\\in B_*,\\ 0<u<\\varepsilon).$$ In particular, $b(s,t)$ has a limit as $(s,t)\\to(s_*,0)$ with $t>0$, without any restriction on the relative rates of approach.*\n\n*Finitely many such functions can use a common ramification and neighborhood. For countably many functions there is a common full-measure set of good points, with the conclusion applied separately to each function; no common ramification or neighborhood is asserted.",
          "source_id": "PM-058.2"
        }
      ],

        "A bounded semialgebraic function",
        "Tangential parameters and positive scale"

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-boundary-data",
          "exact_quote": "In the coordinates of Equation <a href=\"#eq:boundary-graph\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:boundary-graph\">[eq:boundary-graph]</a>, almost every $s_*\\in B$ has the following two properties.*\n\n1.  *There are a neighborhood $B_*\\subset B$ of $s_*$, a number $\\varepsilon>0$, and a real-analytic map $\\psi:B_*\\times(-\\varepsilon,\\varepsilon)\\to\\mathbb R^k$ such that $$\\psi(s,0)=0,\\qquad\n     \\psi(s,u)\\in A_s\\quad(s\\in B_*,\\ 0<u<\\varepsilon).$$*\n\n\n2.  *Set $$E(s,t)=\\mathbb R^k\\setminus t^{-1}A_s,\n     \\qquad d(s,t;v)=\\min\\{1,\\operatorname{dist}(v,E(s,t))\\}.$$ There is a $1$-Lipschitz function $d_*:\\mathbb R^k\\to[0,1]$ such that, for every compact $L\\subset\\mathbb R^k$, $$\\lim_{\\substack{s\\to s_*\\\\t\\downarrow0}}\n           \\sup_{v\\in L}|d(s,t;v)-d_*(v)|=0.\n     \\label{eq:joint-offset-limit}$$ The set $F=\\{v:d_*(v)=0\\}$ is a closed cone, invariant under every positive real dilation, and $0\\in F$.*\n\n*In particular, for every sequence $s_j\\to s_*$, $t_j\\downarrow0$, and $v_j\\to v$, one has $d(s_j,t_j;v_j)\\to d_*(v)$. If $v\\in F$, then $$\\operatorname{dist}(v_j,E(s_j,t_j))\\longrightarrow0.$$ If $v\\notin F$, some fixed ball about $v$ is contained in $t_j^{-1}A_{s_j}$ for all sufficiently large $j$.",
          "source_id": "PM-058.2"
        }

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-smooth-symmetric-domain**: A bounded connected semialgebraic open subset of a complex affine variety with the stated properly discontinuous cocompact holomorphic action is smooth and biholomorphic to a bounded symmetric domain.
- **invariant-proper-intrinsic-distance**: The intrinsic chain-of-discs distance induces the original topology, and its closed balls are compact.
- **invariant-joint-boundary-limits**: At a good boundary parameter, the truncated distances to scaled excluded fibers converge locally uniformly while the tangential parameter and the scale vary together. Their limiting zero set is a closed cone.
- **invariant-supported-noncollapse**: The selected good boundary point carries independent holomorphic supports, and its excluded cone is a proper subset of the normal space. Interior slice balls establish this noncollapse.
- **invariant-two-sided-scaled-convergence**: Compact subsets of the proposed open limit eventually belong to the scaled domains. Every forbidden limit point can also be approached by excluded points along any sequence of scales tending to the boundary.
- **invariant-global-quadratic-equivalence**: The quadratic model is globally biholomorphic to the original domain. Independent normal functionals are strictly positive on the cone and make the Hermitian forms coercive; the original action transfers with proper discontinuity and compact quotient.
- **invariant-divisible-model-symmetry**: A divisible connected model with a bounded realization, the specified translation, rotation and dilation invariances, and a point with vanishing tangential coordinates is biholomorphic to a bounded symmetric domain.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-unverified-global-hypotheses**: Classify an arbitrary bounded holomorphic domain as symmetric from its boundedness alone. -> The classification requires a semialgebraic affine realization and a properly discontinuous action with compact quotient. Boundedness alone does not establish those hypotheses.
- **anti-use-countable-common-ramification**: Choose a common ramification and neighborhood for all rational distance tests in the boundary construction. -> The countable-family assertion supplies a common full-measure set of good points while retaining separate ramifications and neighborhoods. A common neighborhood is not asserted.
- **anti-use-arbitrary-supported-point**: Perform the scaling argument at an arbitrary supported boundary point. -> The proof selects a point where joint boundary data and independent supports hold simultaneously. The preparation does not assert this combination at an arbitrary supported point.
- **anti-use-unregularized-interior-disc**: Treat the limiting unregularized analytic discs as closed discs contained in the domain. -> The proof establishes interior inclusion only for positive regularization. The unregularized discs are used to compute the limiting derivative and may meet the boundary.
- **anti-use-interior-convergence-alone**: Conclude global biholomorphic equivalence from compact interior inclusion in the scaled sets alone. -> Interior inclusion and inverse compactness do not yet locate the entire forward image. Excluded-point convergence is used to rule out unwanted image points and obtain the global inverse identities.
- **anti-use-free-action-restriction**: Reject the classification when the action has nontrivial finite stabilizers, or replace the quotient by a manifold as an extra assumption. -> Freeness is not required, and the transferred action retains the permitted stabilizers. Imposing freeness would unnecessarily narrow the theorem.
- **anti-use-automatic-cone-convexity**: Replace the constructed connected open cone by a convex cone without proof, or make convexity a prerequisite for the final symmetry step. -> The model theorem supplies a connected open cone and strict supports. The stated symmetry theorem uses bounded realization, invariances and divisibility rather than a convexity assumption.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The result classifies arbitrary complex domains through boundary preparation, analytic-disc deformations and limiting holomorphic maps. It is not a finite constructive input-output result with a supplied effective procedure. The proof explicitly uses subsequence extraction: “A diagonal normal-family extraction gives a holomorphic limit $f:U\to\mathbb C^n$.” Finite evaluations of the displayed bounded realization would check only that formula on supplied model data; they would not certify the existence of the global biholomorphism or the symmetry classification.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The classification supplies geometric existence conclusions without an executable procedure for recovering a biholomorphism from a domain description. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The analytic-disc deformations establish interior inclusion in complex domains; they do not specify a simulation update rule or a geometric collision procedure. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The parameter preparation gives local analytic extensions outside exceptional sets without a procedure that selects a good parameter or computes its neighborhood. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The supported-point construction selects a maximizing parameter through positive-measure and null-set arguments, without a finite search contract or a termination bound. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The rank dichotomy describes geometric boundary cells and their algebraic containment; it does not define a storage representation or operations on such a representation. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The disc construction solves an equation in a Sobolev space using bounded operators and contraction arguments, without a finite discretization or hardware execution model. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Properly discontinuous holomorphic actions and compact quotients are geometric hypotheses; the paper provides no adversarial model, cryptographic construction, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The limiting distance function controls joint boundary scaling, but the paper supplies no learning objective, training procedure, or statistical guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The weighted maximum is an existence device on a compact geometric set; no optimization algorithm, stopping criterion, or complexity guarantee is established. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The compact-covering group action concerns holomorphic automorphisms of a domain and does not specify communication, replication, scheduling, or agreement protocols. |
| other (Complex geometric coordinate transformations) | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Given the quadratic model and its independent supporting functionals, the explicit Cayley coordinate transformation constructs a bounded realization with a holomorphic inverse. This is adaptable as a mathematical coordinate contract; obtaining the model and its supports from an arbitrary input domain remains an existence result. |

If $v\\in F$, then $$\\operatorname{dist}(v_j,E(s_j,t_j))\\longrightarrow0.$$ If $v\\notin F$, some fixed ball about $v$ is contained in $t_j^{-1}A_{s_j}$ for all sufficiently large $j$.",
          "source_object": "stmt-prop-boundary-data"
        }

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-affine-bounded-semialgebraic-domain** (hypothesis): The classification requires a nonempty connected semialgebraic bounded open subset of a complex affine variety. Blocked use: ['Apply the classification to an unbounded domain, a general transcendental domain, or an open subset of an unspecified complex space.']
- **limit-proper-cocompact-action** (hypothesis): The holomorphic group action must be properly discontinuous and have compact quotient. Blocked use: ['Deduce symmetry from boundedness and semialgebraicity without proving the action hypotheses.', 'Replace compactness of the quotient by an unproved weaker finiteness condition.']
- **limit-singleton-positive-dimensional-split** (edge_case): The singleton case is handled separately; the boundary and quadratic-model constructions concern positive dimension. Blocked use: ['Apply the positive-dimensional boundary construction to a singleton.']
- **limit-generic-parameter-only** (hypothesis): Ramified analytic extensions and the boundary data are generic local conclusions, rather than assertions at every parameter. Blocked use: ['Choose an arbitrary boundary parameter and assume that the required analytic extensions and joint limits hold there.']
- **limit-countable-family-uniformity** (formal_scope): Countably many functions share a full-measure set of good points, but need not share a ramification or neighborhood. Blocked use: ['Choose a common ramification and neighborhood for all rational distance tests.']
- **limit-joint-moving-parameter-convergence** (hypothesis): Boundary convergence must retain simultaneous variation of the tangential parameter and the normal scale. Blocked use: ['Substitute convergence at a fixed tangential parameter for the joint convergence required by weighted scaling.']
- **limit-supported-point-selection** (nonconstructive): The supported good point is selected by a positive-measure argument in critical fibers; the proof supplies no finite search bound for finding that point. Blocked use: ['Treat an arbitrary supported point or a numerical maximizer as a certified good point with independent supports.']
- **limit-disc-family-antecedents** (hypothesis): The interior-ball construction requires real-analytic approaching interior graphs and the continuity principle for the relevant disc deformations. Blocked use: ['Infer included balls from approaching sets without the analytic graph and disc-continuity hypotheses.']
- **limit-positive-disc-regularization** (edge_case): Interior inclusion is proved for positively regularized discs; the unregularized family serves the limiting derivative calculation. Blocked use: ['Use an unregularized closed disc as an interior disc without an additional inclusion proof.']
- **limit-fixed-disc-parameter-choices** (formal_scope): The allowed displacement size and included-ball constants are fixed after the auxiliary disc parameters; uniformity as the auxiliary parameter decreases is not asserted. Blocked use: ['Treat the included-ball constants as uniform while varying the auxiliary disc parameter.']
- **limit-two-sided-scaling-required** (hypothesis): Compact interior inclusion alone does not identify the entire image of the limiting map; excluded-point convergence is also used. Blocked use: ['Conclude global biholomorphic equivalence from interior scaled-set inclusion alone.']
- **limit-noneffective-global-map** (nonconstructive): The global forward and inverse maps are obtained through normal-family compactness and subsequence extraction, without a finite recovery procedure or a complexity bound. Blocked use: ['Advertise automatic recognition of the symmetric realization from an arbitrary domain description.', 'Interpret finite evaluations of model formulas as certification of the global classification.']
- **limit-bounded-realization-input-data** (hypothesis): The explicit bounded coordinate transformation uses an already supplied Hermitian model and independent supporting functionals satisfying the strict positivity inequalities. Blocked use: ['Evaluate the bounded coordinate formula on arbitrary quadratic data and claim the stated boundedness and invertibility conclusions.']
- **limit-vey-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The final symmetry theorem requires a connected domain with a bounded realization, the stated invariances, a point with vanishing tangential coordinates, and divisibility. Blocked use: ['Infer symmetry from a quadratic defining expression without checking bounded realization and divisibility.']
- **limit-no-extra-freeness-or-algebraicity** (formal_scope): The scope allows finite stabilizers and does not require the acting biholomorphisms to be algebraic. Blocked use: ['Reject an admissible action solely because it has finite stabilizers.', 'Add algebraicity of the acting biholomorphisms as a required hypothesis.']
- **limit-cone-convexity-not-input** (formal_scope): The constructed cone is connected and open; convexity of its defining cone and regularity of its boundary are not inputs to the invoked symmetry theorem. Blocked use: ['Replace the constructed cone by a convex cone without proof.', 'Require cone convexity before applying the stated symmetry theorem.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 058 (`lean/docs/058.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Symmetry of semialgebraic bounded domains with compact quotient](../../preprints/Symmetry-of-semialgebraic-bounded-domains-with-compact-quotient-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalized result answers the paper's symmetry question affirmatively. A nonempty connected bounded semialgebraic subset, relatively open in a complex affine algebraic set, is smooth and biholomorphic to a bounded symmetric domain whenever a discrete group acts properly and holomorphically with compact quotient.
>
> The action need not be free, and the ambient algebraic set may be singular.

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `Affine` | abbrev |
| `realCoordinates` | def |
| `IsAffineAlgebraic` | def |
| `IsSemialgebraic` | inductive |
| `HolomorphicOnSubset` | def |
| `Biholomorph` | structure |
| `IsSmooth` | def |
| `IsBoundedSymmetricDomain` | def |
| `main` | theorem |

Comparator source `SymmetricDomains.lean` (2704 bytes, sha256 `2d838d21d7431014cea2d5af5bc4b64121075333c5833b4cfc5efb73c4a1f1ce`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace Release061

abbrev Affine (n : ℕ) := Fin n → ℂ

def realCoordinates {n : ℕ} (z : Affine n) : Fin n ⊕ Fin n → ℝ :=
  Sum.elim (fun j => (z j).re) (fun j => (z j).im)

def IsAffineAlgebraic {n : ℕ} (V : Set (Affine n)) : Prop :=
  ∃ P : Set (MvPolynomial (Fin n) ℂ),
    V = {z | ∀ p ∈ P, MvPolynomial.eval z p = 0}

inductive IsSemialgebraic {n : ℕ} : Set (Affine n) → Prop
  | zero (p : MvPolynomial (Fin n ⊕ Fin n) ℝ) :
      IsSemialgebraic {z | MvPolynomial.eval (realCoordinates z) p = 0}
  | positive (p : MvPolynomial (Fin n ⊕ Fin n) ℝ) :
      IsSemialgebraic {z | 0 < MvPolynomial.eval (realCoordinates z) p}
  | compl {S : Set (Affine n)} : IsSemialgebraic S → IsSemialgebraic Sᶜ
  | union {S T : Set (Affine n)} :
      IsSemialgebraic S → IsSemialgebraic T → IsSemialgebraic (S ∪ T)

def HolomorphicOnSubset {n m : ℕ} (S : Set (Affine n)) (f : S → Affine m) : Prop :=
  ∀ p : S, ∃ W : Set (Affine n), IsOpen W ∧ (p : Affine n) ∈ W ∧
    ∃ F : Affine n → Affine m, AnalyticOnNhd ℂ F W ∧
      ∀ q : S, (q : Affine n) ∈ W → F q = f q

structure Biholomorph {n m : ℕ} (S : Set (Affine n)) (T : Set (Affine m)) where
  toHomeomorph : S ≃ₜ T
  holomorphic_toFun : HolomorphicOnSubset S (fun p => (toHomeomorph p : Affine m))
  holomorphic_invFun : HolomorphicOnSubset T (fun q => (toHomeomorph.symm q : Affine n))

def IsSmooth {n : ℕ} (S : Set (Affine n)) : Prop :=
  ∀ p : S, ∃ (m : ℕ) (W : Set (Affine n)), W ⊆ S ∧
    IsOpen ((Subtype.val : S → Affine n) ⁻¹' W) ∧ (p : Affine n) ∈ W ∧
    ∃ D : Set (Affine m), IsOpen D ∧ Nonempty (Biholomorph W D)

def IsBoundedSymmetricDomain {n : ℕ} (D : Set (Affine n)) : Prop :=
  IsOpen D ∧ IsConnected D ∧ Bornology.IsBounded D ∧
    ∀ p : D, ∃ σ : Biholomorph D D,
      Function.Involutive σ.toHomeomorph ∧ σ.toHomeomorph p = p ∧
      ∃ W : Set D, IsOpen W ∧ p ∈ W ∧
        ∀ q ∈ W, σ.toHomeomorph q = q → q = p

theorem main {n : ℕ} (V U : Set (Affine n))
    (hV : IsAffineAlgebraic V) (hUV : U ⊆ V)
    (hopen : IsOpen ((Subtype.val : V → Affine n) ⁻¹' U))
    (hconn : IsConnected U) (hsa : IsSemialgebraic U)
    (hbounded : Bornology.IsBounded U)
    (Γ : Type*) [Group Γ] [TopologicalSpace Γ] [DiscreteTopology Γ]
    [MulAction Γ U] [ProperSMul Γ U]
    [CompactSpace (Quotient (MulAction.orbitRel Γ U))]
    (hhol : ∀ γ : Γ, HolomorphicOnSubset U (fun p => (γ • p : U).val)) :
    IsSmooth U ∧ ∃ (m : ℕ) (D : Set (Affine m)),
      IsBoundedSymmetricDomain D ∧ Nonempty (Biholomorph U D) := by
  sorry

end Release061

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Every nonempty connected semialgebraic bounded open subset of a complex affine variety admitting a properly discontinuous cocompact group of biholomorphisms is smooth and biholomorphic to a bounded symmetric domain. This answers the bounded-domain question of Kollár and Pardon affirmatively.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [stmt-thm-main](#stmt-thm-main) | theorem | `thm:main` | 1 | Let $U$ be a nonempty connected semialgebraic bounded open subset of a complex affine variety. |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A bounded symmetric domain is a bounded domain in a complex vector space with a holomorphic involution at every point for which that point is an isolated fixed point. Such domains are distinguished by their large automorphism groups. A compact quotient imposes a different kind of uniformity: every point can be moved into a fixed compact set, but the automorphism group need not be assumed transitive. We show that a semialgebraic realization converts this weaker uniformity into symmetry.

We regard an affine variety as a reduced complex algebraic set, and fix a closed algebraic embedding into some $\mathbb C^N$. A subset is semialgebraic if it is given by a finite Boolean combination of polynomial equalities and inequalities in the real and imaginary coordinates. Boundedness is in this embedding. The open set below is open in the complex-space topology; it need not initially be a manifold.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $U$ be a nonempty connected semialgebraic bounded open subset of a complex affine variety. Suppose that a group $\Gamma$ of biholomorphisms acts properly discontinuously on $U$ and that $U/\Gamma$ is compact. Then $U$ is smooth and is biholomorphic to a bounded symmetric domain.*

</div>

Proper discontinuity is understood as properness of the action when $\Gamma$ has the discrete topology. Finite stabilizers are allowed. No smoothness or projectivity assumption is made on the quotient. We use the usual nonempty-domain convention; a connected zero-dimensional $U$ is a point and gives the zero-dimensional case of the theorem.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
###### The problem and its predecessors.

Kollár and Pardon posed the bounded semialgebraic affine-domain question in their study of algebraic varieties with semialgebraic universal covers <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[KP2012](#ref-KP2012), arXiv version 2, Question 25]. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> answers that question affirmatively. It concerns the geometry of the domain itself: the affine ambient variety may be singular, and the compact quotient may have finite quotient singularities. The semialgebraic hypothesis describes the domain in one affine embedding; it imposes no algebraicity requirement on the biholomorphisms in $\Gamma$. This bounded-domain question is distinct from the broader universal-cover classification proposed in the same paper. The companion article <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[OpenAISemialgebraicCovers2026](#ref-OpenAISemialgebraicCovers2026), Theorem 1.1] establishes that classification for ordinary universal covers of connected normal projective complex varieties. The proof below is independent of that classification.

The question arises when one tries to pass from quasi-projective universal covers to semialgebraic ones. As Kollár and Pardon explain <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[KP2012](#ref-KP2012), arXiv version 2, Section 3, p. 13], when a free properly discontinuous action on a bounded open subset of a Stein manifold has compact quotient, that quotient is projective and its canonical bundle is ample. Consequently, the argument that excludes varieties of general type in the quasi-projective-cover setting does not extend to this case. The bounded-domain question asks what geometry replaces that exclusion.

Earlier rigidity theorems explain why boundary geometry is a natural way to approach the problem. Wong’s characterization of the ball and Rosay’s subsequent localization theorem show that a bounded domain with an automorphism orbit accumulating at a $C^2$ strongly pseudoconvex boundary point is biholomorphic to a ball <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Wong1977](#ref-Wong1977), [Rosay1979](#ref-Rosay1979)]. Rosay’s argument uses a holomorphic peak function to force an entire sequence of automorphisms toward the same boundary point <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Rosay1979](#ref-Rosay1979), Section 3, Lemma 2]. The first step of our proof extends this localization mechanism to an open subset of a possibly singular, nonnormal affine variety. A polynomial factor first excludes the ambient singular locus, and bounded holomorphic functions on finite branched charts provide the compactness needed before smoothness has been established.

Frankel proved that a convex hyperbolic domain with compact quotient is biholomorphic to a bounded symmetric domain <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Frankel1989](#ref-Frankel1989), Theorem 1]. He also treated a broader condition on the boundary, called $h$-convexity <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Frankel1989](#ref-Frankel1989), Theorem 2.6 and Definition 7.3]. His proof relates compact-quotient rigidity to limits of rescaled automorphisms <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Frankel1989](#ref-Frankel1989), Section 3]. Although his initial theorem is stated for a free action, he explicitly removes that restriction later in the paper <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Frankel1989](#ref-Frankel1989), p. 144, following Lemma 11.8]. Thus finite stabilizers are part of the earlier rigidity picture as well. The issue here is to extract the appropriate model from semialgebraicity without assuming convexity or a boundary regularity condition. In the setting of a bounded domain covering a compact complex manifold, Zimmer proved that a $C^{1,1}$ boundary already forces the domain to be a ball <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Zimmer2021](#ref-Zimmer2021), Theorem 1.1]. That regularity theorem and the present semialgebraic theorem apply under different hypotheses.

An important earlier rigidity theorem is due to Vey: a divisible generalized Siegel domain is symmetric <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Vey1970](#ref-Vey1970), Theorem 1 and the following paragraph]. This class was introduced by Kaup, Matsushima, and Ochiai, as Vey recalls at the start of his paper. Here divisibility means a properly discontinuous action with a compact covering set; it does not require a free action. The present problem is to reach a domain to which that theorem applies without assuming a regular boundary, convexity, or homogeneity. We do so by a rescaling construction and verify Vey’s hypotheses explicitly in Section <a href="#sec:symmetry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:symmetry">6</a>.

Semialgebraicity provides finite geometric descriptions, but it does not make the relevant boundary a smooth hypersurface. Corners may have several complex-normal directions, and their normal fibers can shrink at rates that depend on the tangential parameters. The proof consequently uses compatible Nash cell decomposition and semialgebraic selection <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Coste2005](#ref-Coste2005), Sections 1.1–1.2], followed by a parameter argument that keeps the tangential variables and the scale together. Analytic discs then turn a family of approaching interior graphs into balls with a definite size after dilation. This is the step that prevents the normal limit from collapsing.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
###### The boundary construction.

In positive dimension, the essential intermediate result is a global equivalence, for integers $m\geq0$ and $k\geq1$ with $m+k=\dim_\mathbb CU$, $$U\simeq
 \{(z,w)\in\mathbb C^m\times\mathbb C^k:
        \mathop{\mathrm{Im}}w-H(z,z)\in C\},$$ where $C\subset\mathbb R^k$ is a nonempty connected open cone and $H:\mathbb C^m\times\mathbb C^m\to\mathbb C^k$ is a vector of Hermitian forms. Independent real linear functionals on the normal variables are strictly positive on $C$ and give positive-definite scalar Hermitian forms after composition with $H$. The displayed domain is invariant under real translations of $w$, scalar unit rotations of $z$, and the weighted dilations $(z,w)\mapsto(\sqrt r\,z,rw)$, $r>0$. The support inequalities give a bounded realization in $\mathbb C^{m+k}$. Together with the transferred compact quotient, these are precisely the properties used in Vey’s theorem. They are conclusions of the construction.

Two features of that construction are useful beyond a smooth hypersurface setting. First, semialgebraicity supplies a boundary stratum at which normal-offset distance functions have limits jointly in the tangential parameter and the scale. A measure argument selects such a point together with enough independent holomorphic supports to control every complex-normal direction. Second, analytic discs produce interior slice balls whose radius is comparable to their distance from that point. This rules out collapse of the normal limit even when the boundary has higher real codimension. Both statements are formulated with the parameter uniformity needed for weighted rescaling.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### Proof outline.

Section <a href="#sec:geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:geometry">2</a> first removes the singularities of $U$. A polynomial-weighted maximum gives a peak at a smooth ambient point; cocompactness moves each point of $U$ into that smooth neighborhood. The same compact-covering property makes the intrinsic chain-of-discs distance proper and gives a continuity principle for analytic-disc deformations.

Section <a href="#sec:preparation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preparation">3</a> constructs the supported boundary point and the joint normal-offset limits. Section <a href="#sec:noncollapse" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:noncollapse">4</a> proves that the limiting interior contains an open cone. In Section <a href="#sec:scaling" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:scaling">5</a>, tangential dilation by $t^{-1/2}$ and normal dilation by $t^{-1}$ yield the quadratic model. The proof uses both interior and excluded-point convergence: normal-family limits of the maps and their inverses give a global biholomorphism, not merely an embedding into a candidate model. Section <a href="#sec:symmetry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:symmetry">6</a> then applies Vey’s theorem and completes the proof.

Figure <a href="#fig:proof" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof">1</a> records where the two boundary inputs enter. The independent supports control the scaled automorphisms in every normal direction; the interior balls establish that there is a nonempty model to which their inverses can be applied. Both sides of the set convergence are then needed to identify the entire image of the limiting map.

<figure id="fig:proof">

<figcaption>The two boundary inputs have different roles. Peaks bound the forward scaling maps, while discs ensure a nonempty normal interior. The two-sided convergence then identifies the global quadratic model. </figcaption>
</figure>
